| E120 | Where the Product lives becomes a repo fact merged on its own, not a line the PR writes — **fixed** (2026-09-27). **The stated limit E117 left**, the half E119 could not close: the four Product-reading gates took `product-repo` from `link.md` on the base, then a sibling, and on a repo's first spec the PR's own value, so lineage-check, epic-open and reconcile-debt-check still deferred on a first spec pointing nowhere. **Found first:** the shipped CI workflows never checked the Product out, and no doc said how — so in every standard two-repo setup the gates could never reach it in CI, and since E119 a first spec with a slice failed with advice nobody could follow. **The user's decisions (2026-09-27): (1) ship a CI checkout step** that uses a named secret, `YAD_PRODUCT_TOKEN`; without it the step is skipped and the gates defer as before. **(2) The record is `.sdlc/product-link.json`** in the code repo — `git_url`, `path` (from the repo root; where CI checks the Product out, `.yad/product` by default) and `default_branch`. The first choice, `.sdlc/product.json`, was withdrawn before building: that is the Product's own settings file (shape 3's name for `hub.json`), and ~20 commands take a folder holding it for a Product. **Written by `yad check --fix` / `yad update`** (there is no `yad connect-repos` command): the Product knows both sides, and `--push` commits it to each repo's default branch. **(3) With neither the record nor a base `link.md`, today's order is kept** for a major; `yad doctor` warns `repos:product-link-missing`. **(4) `link.md`'s `product-repo` is kept, and the record wins** with a note — printed only when `link.md` reaches a different folder here, since a value written on its author's machine reaches nothing in CI. **Built:** the shared Product block (byte-identical in all four gates) reads the record's `path` from the base before anything else; a record the PR adds or changes counts once it merges, with a note; a merged record that reaches nothing defers (not E119's first-spec FAIL — it is a reviewed fact, not the PR's choice). `checks/product-checkout.sh` (wired into every code repo, run before each of the four gates in both workflows) reads the record from the base and clones the Product's default branch into `path` when the secret is set; a path outside the repo, `.`, under `.git` or holding tracked files FAILs, and so does a clone that fails with a token set; the token reaches git through its environment as an HTTP header, never a command line or URL, and GitHub masks its encoded form. The writer refreshes `git_url` and `default_branch` from the Product each time and keeps `path` and any other key the team set; a code repo inside the Product's own git repo (a monorepo) gets none; an unreadable record is left alone unless `--overwrite-local`, which saves it first; on an already-wired repo it is `new`, so `yad update` adds it. `yad doctor` also warns `checks:product-record-blind` for a gate that never reads the record or a hand-changed workflow that never runs the checkout. `yad-spec` writes `../../<the record's path>` into a new `link.md`. **Review 1 found and fixed:** a record written by `yad setup` or a plain `--fix` read `ok` and was never committed by a later `--push` — the gates read it from the default branch, and the doctor only looked at the disk; an uncommitted record is now `new`, and the doctor asks the default branch (and only of wired repos, since its hint would install a repo's whole wiring). A token in the Product's `git_url` was copied into every code repo — the user name and password are now taken out. On a developer's machine the record's path reaches nothing, so every local gate run deferred — when it reaches nothing, the base's `link.md` order is used if it reaches a folder. A PR could track `.yad` as a symlink and send the authenticated clone elsewhere — a symlink on the way FAILs. A Product with no `default_branch` got a guessed `main` (a `master` trunk failed every clone) — it is left empty, and the clone takes the remote's default; an `ssh://` port is dropped from the https url. The docs now say who can read the token (anyone who can push a branch) and that a protected GitLab variable does not reach feature-branch pipelines. **Review 2 found and fixed:** the same gap on its other arm — a committed record that a plain `--fix` rewrote on disk (the Product moved) read `ok` again, so `--push` never committed the change and CI kept the old url; a record is now `ok` only when committed as it stands (`git diff HEAD` also sees a staged-only change), and the doctor compares the default branch's copy with the disk. A repo whose `.gitignore` covers `.sdlc/` could never commit the record, and both tools nagged forever with a hint that could not work — the writer leaves it `ok` and the doctor names the ignore rule. Smaller: a tracked folder at the checkout path read as "already there" and passed (the tracked check now runs first); a `?token=` query is stripped from `git_url`; an `http://` url FAILs by name (the token is not sent over plain http); the PR-changed-record note no longer claims the base's record is what is read. **Review 3 found and fixed:** a `--push` whose push was refused (a protected default branch) left the record committed locally and `ok` for the writer, while the doctor kept telling the user to run `--fix --push`, which had nothing left to commit — the doctor now names a record committed but not pushed and gives the push (or a PR) as the fix; a default branch copy that is not JSON is called unreadable, not absent. **Review 4:** "not pushed" was decided by the copies alone, so a record pushed to a feature branch (`--allow-branch`) and a clone behind origin (a teammate moved the checkout path) both got a `git push origin main` that did nothing or was refused — "not pushed" now needs the clone on the default branch with a commit to the record origin lacks; the feature branch case names the branch and a PR as the fix, and the behind case says pull. **Review 5** found the same shape again (a feature branch merely cut before a record change on main was told to open a PR; a diverged clone was told to push; a detached HEAD fell back to the `--fix --push` dead end), so the case-by-case rules were replaced by one table decided by git: no commit to the record in `origin/<b>..HEAD` → behind (pull on `<b>`, nothing elsewhere); with one → not pushed, diverged, detached or on another branch. **Review 6** confirmed every row, following each hint to a clean doctor, and found the table's one silent spot: off the default branch, "not ahead" was taken as "CI has the newer record" even when the record on the default branch had been deleted or broken — silence is now kept only while that record is readable. The older `--fix --push` hint (missing, unreadable, stale) names the branch switch when the clone is on another branch, since `--push` commits only on the default branch; the pull hint names `origin <branch>`. **Review 7:** following those hints end to end cleared every case but one — a record that is not JSON, once pulled, is left alone by a plain `--fix`, so the hint now names `--overwrite-local` for it; and the per-repo hint reads one item per repo (`backend (on feat/y): git checkout main && git pull origin main`), "a detached HEAD" rather than "HEAD", with the pull folded into the switch. **Review 8:** `--overwrite-local` was asked for whenever the default branch's copy was unreadable, even when the disk copy was already good — it is now asked for only when a plain `--fix` would leave the copy alone; and the hint says the flag applies to the whole run (every connected repo), not to one repo; the branch switch now reads first. **Review 9:** for a clone off the default branch the flag was judged on today's copy, though the hint's first step (the switch) replaces a record the branch tracks — now judged on the copy the clone will have after it. **CI (e2e) found** that review 1's fix — an uncommitted record reads `new` — made a fresh `yad setup` report drift, where every other wired file setup leaves uncommitted reads `ok`; the record now reads `ok` on content and carries `pendingCommit`, which `--push` stages anyway. **Stated:** a repo whose CI has no `YAD_PRODUCT_TOKEN` still defers, as before; the checkout needs git 2.31+ on the runner. Tests: the record wins over `link.md` in all four gates on a first spec; a different-folder note; a record added or changed in the PR is not read; a merged record reaching nothing defers; the checkout script with and without the secret, from the base only, each refused path, a failed clone; the workflows run it before each gate; the writer's create/refresh/keep-path, `yad update` adding it, the monorepo skip, the unreadable record; both doctor warnings **Shipped (#286).** | M | E117 + E119 |
| E121 | contract-check folds only the long s — **fixed** (2026-09-27). **Left by E116's reviews 4–5, older than it.** E115's second-spelling rules fold names as a Mac does, but knew ASCII case and `ſ` only; APFS uses full case folding, so 13 characters fold into ASCII alone (the Kelvin sign is `k`, `ß`/`ẞ` are `ss`, the ligatures U+FB00–U+FB06 are their letters, and normalization makes U+037E `;` and U+1FEF `` ` ``). **Checked on scratch repos first, the row's claim was out of date:** a twin story folder holding a changed slice (`specs/EP-<Kelvin>iosk-S01/contracts/…`) already FAILs, because E117's strict story ID refuses the folder — no lock check is deferred. What was left: a twin holding other files (`specs/EP-<Kelvin>iosk-S01/notes.md` beside `specs/EP-kiosk-S01/`) passed, where its ASCII-case twin is refused — the same rule, blind to 12 of the 13 characters. **Fixed:** `fold` is now backfill-check's (all 13, from Unicode's tables). **Its twin, fixed too:** the tree read split `git ls-tree -z` on newlines alone (E116 review 2 fixed the same in backfill-check), so a file named `x<newline>specs` read as a file named `specs`, and a name spelling a whole record read as a symlink under `specs/` — every PR refused, naming a file that is not there; it is now `tr '\n\0' '?\n'`. **Review 1 found the same split in the rest of the script and its three twins:** contract-check's changed and deleted lists (a file named `notes<newline>specs/S1/contracts/api.md` read as a slice change — a false FAIL over a file that does not exist), and four reads in the shared Product block of all four Product-reading gates (sibling `link.md` files, tracked `.sdlc/hub.json` files, links in the base epic folder, tracked parts of the Product path). Every `tr '\0' '\n'` in the four gates is now `tr '\n\0' '?\n'`, so the shared block stays byte-identical. Most only caused a false FAIL; **review 2 found one false PASS:** in the changed list, a slice under a story folder named with a newline (`specs/EP-a<newline>b-S01/contracts/api.md`) split into two lines that matched nothing — an empty surface, and a PASS. It now reads `EP-a?b-S01` and FAILs as not a story ID. Review 2 also checked that the `?` never loosens a gate (the tracked-path test, the sibling and `hub.json` lists, the only-removes hatch). **Stated:** no doctor warning for an older copy — this closes an inconsistency, not a bypass, and the older copy still refuses the ASCII twins and every twin that holds a slice. Other non-ASCII case and NFC/NFD twins stay as E115 states. Tests: a story-folder twin for each folding character, and both newline names **Shipped (#284).** | S | — |
| E113 | Windows without WSL — the CLI and the agent hooks. Left by E43 (2026-09-25): every harness hook was a bash script, and the README and `docs/CLI.md` said WSL only. **The decisions were the user's (2026-09-27).** **(1) Scope:** the hooks plus the Windows problems in the CLI they reach, proved by ONE new CI job on `windows-latest` (Node 18 and 22) that runs `cli/test-hooks.mjs` — a file written to run on every OS. The full suite and the end-to-end harness stay Linux + macOS: they drive bash gates and POSIX paths throughout, and the docs claim only what the Windows job proves. **(2) The hooks are Node scripts on every OS, one design:** `hooks/ledger-guard.mjs`, `hooks/ledger-guard-cursor.mjs`, `hooks/yad-capture.mjs`, dependency-free, each finding the Product from its own location (`import.meta.url`, symlinks resolved) — never from a harness variable, whose form on Windows (`C:\…` or `/c/…`) no harness documents. They share one `yad-resolve` block (YAD_BIN → the Product's `node_modules` run with this Node → `yad` on PATH → `npx --no-install`), kept byte-identical by a test. On Windows an npm launcher is a `.cmd`, which Node will not start without a shell, so a global install's `yad.mjs` beside the launcher is run with this Node instead, and only a bare `.cmd` goes through a shell with every word quoted. **(3) Entries:** Claude Code `node "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/hooks/<x>.mjs"` — shell form, because Claude Code runs hooks in Git Bash on Windows (it falls back to PowerShell without it, where the entry does not run; `yad doctor` finds Git Bash the way Claude Code does, beside `git.exe` or at `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`, and warns when it is missing). Claude Code's exec form (`command` + `args`) was rejected: a version that ignores `args` runs bare `node`, which swallows the payload and exits 0 — a guard silently off. Cursor `node hooks/<x>.mjs` — relative, no variable, no quotes: Cursor documents a project hook as running from the project root and calls the command a "shell string" (PowerShell on Windows, by community report), and shows `python3 x.py`. Capture's format is passed by the entry (`--format claude`), no longer sniffed from `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, which Cursor also sets. **(4) The old scripts are removed:** each adapter lists its bash command in `legacyCommands`, so `yad check --fix` / `yad update` rewrite the entry in place, and a new `removed` action deletes `hooks/*.sh` — only a copy `.sdlc/managed.json` proves yad wrote and nobody edited, and only when no settings file names it: not as COMMITTED (HEAD — the rewritten entry is the team's to commit, `--push` never stages it, so deleting first would hand everyone who pulls a hook running a missing script), and not on disk as this run's hook actions will leave the files (each adapter's own entry merged or unmerged in memory, exactly as the actions do — review 2: excusing our old commands everywhere also excused copies no action reaches, under another event or in `settings.local.json`, which were then planned, kept and pending for ever). Both are read at plan time, and a script waiting only on the commit says so, so `yad check` never shows a removal `--fix` would then skip; `.claude/settings.local.json` is read too, and either slash matches. **Windows fixes in the CLI:** Node reports no execute bit on Windows, so every `exec` gate script read `outdated` for ever — `isExecutable` is true there (CI runs the gates as `bash checks/<gate>.sh`), and a hook needs no bit at all now; a CRLF checkout (`core.autocrlf`, on by default there) made every managed file read `outdated`/`modified` — the planner compares with `contentSha`, CRLF counted as LF, identical to `fileSha` for an LF file so every recorded sha still matches (artifact hashes stay byte-exact); the detached capture push and fetch pass `windowsHide`, or each would flash a console window; `npx` (repomix) and `npm` (`yad docs build`/`deploy`) run through a shell on Windows (`runLauncher`), since an npm launcher is a `.cmd` — its arguments quoted, its name bare: a `.cmd` reached on PATH by a quoted name reads `%~dp0` as the current folder, so `"npx"` looked for its own script inside the repo being packed (#326, fixed after 4.5.0); a payload path in URL form (`/c:/…`, reported from Cursor) is read as the Windows path it is, or the guard would allow the edit. **Line endings:** artifact hashes stay byte-exact on purpose (an approval binds bytes), so a CRLF checkout reads approvals as stale — `yad doctor` warns on Windows when `core.autocrlf=true` and names `core.autocrlf input`; making the hash line-ending-blind would change what an approval means, and is not done here. **Review 1 found and fixed:** `--push` could commit the scripts' deletion without the settings change (the HEAD check above); a kept script was pending on every `yad check` (the plan-time check); `yad docs` ran `npm` without a shell; the docs claimed the whole CLI. **Review 2 found and fixed:** the excused-command list above; the CRLF remedy joined commands with `&&`, which Windows PowerShell 5.1 does not have. **Review 3 (nothing serious):** a script kept by our own old command that no action reaches is now named with the way out; the commit-wait line reads the committed files once per run; the apply-time warning names `yad check --fix` (reachable from `yad setup`, which does not unwire a dropped target). The two notes are `info` lines, not warnings, so a `--json` caller sees them on stderr only. **Review 4 (nothing serious):** on a Product that is not verified, a stuck old guard entry is told to be removed — no guard script is installed there to point it at. **Review 5:** that advice was only on the plan's note, not its twin, apply's warning — both now come from one `entryAdvice`, which also says when the config does not read and counts the entries; tests pin which script gets which advice. **Review 6:** apply's warning still sent a local-ledger guard entry to `yad check --fix`, which leaves it alone; the advice is now one count-free phrase per case, used whole at both sites. **Review 7:** the repoint phrase promised `yad check --fix` would do it — false for exactly the entries that reach it — and a dropped harness should lose its entry, not be repointed; it now says "point … at the `node hooks/….mjs` command, or remove it", and a test pins every branch's full text. **Review 8:** the capture twin of review 4 — with `"capture": false` no Node capture script is installed, so a stuck capture entry is told to be removed. **Not proved on Windows:** the rest of the CLI, the full suite, the e2e harness, a machine with no Git Bash under Claude Code, and whether `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` reaches the entry in a form Git Bash hands to Node intact (the scripts do not depend on it past finding themselves). **No shape change.** **Shipped (#287).** | M | E43 |
| E44 | Step-boundary fold into one clean commit — shipped as **`yad fold <epic> <step>`**. The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-25). **(1) The draft branch is left alone.** The fold does not delete, reset or mark `yad/wip/<name>/<epic>`: E95 reads the draft history, and deleting a capture branch is the multi-machine trap E43's reviews kept finding (a machine holding a copy pushes it back). Capture's own rule makes the next capture a no-op — the folded files equal both the new HEAD and the branch tip, and the branch's `Yad-Base` is no longer HEAD. **(2) Only this step's files:** `artifactPaths()` — what the step's review covers (architecture = `architecture.md`, `contract.md`, `.sdlc/contract-lock.json`; stories = all of `stories/`) — plus what the step's skill commits beside them (`DESIGN.md` and `.sdlc/design-links.json` for `ui-design`, `.sdlc/test-links.json` for `test-cases`). The epic's other changed artifacts are named and left on disk, so another step's half-written draft never lands in this step's commit. **(3) Trigger:** the command. The authoring skills run it where they used to say "author and commit"; `yad open-pr` on a `review/EP-*` branch only WARNS about step files no fold has committed. **(4) The ledger follows `ledger` mode:** `local` — the epic's changed ledger files go in the SAME commit, one step boundary, one commit; `verified` — CI writes the ledger at merge, so ledger changes are left for CI, except the new ledger files of an epic with no `.sdlc/state.json` in HEAD — its seed, which is exactly what `ledger-guard` exempts (an epic's ledger only while its state.json is off the base ref: creation, not mutation, #162) and the only path a seed has to the default branch. While the epic is new, its new person-written `.sdlc/` files (a change-epic's `change.json` and pointer `contract-lock.json`) ride that fold too: the seed is one thing. **Defaults taken:** a real foreground `git commit --only` with the paths — the fold is the commit that is signed, so it honours `commit.gpgsign` and the person's commit hooks, and anything else they staged stays staged and uncommitted; new files are `git add`ed first (only the step's paths). One quiet capture runs first, so the draft branch holds what is folded. The message is `docs(<epic>): author <step>` with `Yad-Epic`, `Yad-Step` and `Yad-Folded` (the capture branch's tip, or `none` with capture off) — the pointer E95 reads. Refused on the default branch with `ledger: verified` (artifacts reach it only through the review PR); allowed anywhere with `ledger: local`. Refused when the step's files have no changes. `--json` like every command. **A finding, fixed here:** `yad-ui` and `yad-test-cases` commit `.sdlc/design-links.json` and `.sdlc/test-links.json` as "artifact-side, not ledger", but E43's capture treated them as ledger and never saved them; capture now takes them too. **Reviews 1–5 found and fixed:** a deletion already staged (`git rm`, the old half of a `git mv`) is in neither the index nor on disk, so it was dropped and a renamed story reached the review twice — it now goes to `commit --only` (it is in HEAD) and never to `git add`; `git add -A` given an EMPTY path list stages every change in the repository, so it is skipped when there is nothing to add; a merge, cherry-pick, revert or rebase in progress is refused before anything is staged; a detached HEAD is refused with a verified ledger; a `git rm --cached` path (a staged deletion git also lists as untracked) is refused by name, never quietly added back; on a case-blind disk (macOS, Windows) the old name of a case-only rename also "exists", so a staged deletion still on disk is refused as a case-only rename only when git shows the new spelling staged, and otherwise neutrally — never with a "delete the file" hint, which would delete the renamed one; `yad open-pr` on a per-story review branch warns only about its own story. **A last check against the gates it meets later:** the first rule folded ANY new ledger file in verified mode, but `ledger-guard` exempts one only while its epic is unseeded, so a hand-written `reviews/*.md` in a seeded epic would have failed the review PR — the rule now asks the guard's own question of HEAD; a change-epic's `change.json` belonged to no step and would have been left on disk for ever; and with a verified ledger the fold warns when commit signing is off (`verified-commits` would fail the review PR). **No shape change.** **Shipped (#276).** | M | E43 |
| E46 | File claims — record, show, expire — shipped as **`yad claims`** and a warning from the capture hook. The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-25). **(1) Record is E43, with nothing added:** a claim is READ from the capture branches — no claim file, no `yad claim` command, so nobody has to remember anything. A claim is a file of the epic that differs between a `yad/wip/<name>/<epic>` branch's tip and the tip's `Yad-Base` — the person's own in-progress edits. Not the diff against the previous capture: after a pull, a capture's tree is the NEW HEAD plus the edits, and the pull's changes would read as that person's work. When `Yad-Base` is not in this clone (a later HEAD is not an ancestor of the branch, so it was never pushed with it), the commit the branch's FIRST capture was built on is the fallback — it is an ancestor, so it always arrives with the branch — marked `basis: first-capture`: over-reporting, the safe side for advice. **(2) Show:** `yad claims [<epic>]` fetches everyone's capture branches and the default branch first (`--prune`, 30 s, no prompts; `--no-fetch`; `--json`), and the post-edit capture hook warns when an edit changed a file someone ELSE has a claim on — reading only what is already fetched, never blocking, at most once an hour per person and file (kept in the per-clone `yad-capture.json`). Not in `yad status`. **(3) Expiry:** 4 hours after the branch's last save, and at once when the file on the default branch matches the saved one — so a merged fold releases its files even if the person never captures again. **(4) The warning reaches the agent:** under Claude Code the hook prints one PostToolUse JSON note (`additionalContext` for the agent, `systemMessage` for the person) — so `hooks/yad-capture.sh` no longer throws its stdout away, and passes `--format claude` when `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is set; new bytes, so every wired repo reads `outdated` until `yad update`. Cursor's `afterFileEdit` reads nothing back: a line on stderr there. The E43 contract "prints nothing to stdout" now has that one exception. **(5) Freshness:** the hook's throttled background push also starts a detached fetch of everyone's capture branches (no prune from the hook; the 4-hour expiry bounds a deleted branch). **Limits, stated:** capture off or offline is invisible; a claim can be about 5 minutes late; two people with one git name share branches and never see each other; times are the saver's clock. **Review 1 found and fixed:** a push rewrote the per-clone state file with `lastPushAt` alone, wiping the once-an-hour memory, so the warning came back every 5 minutes — the push now keeps the file's other keys; the capture's "files this edit changed" counted a file that only moved because HEAD moved (a pull, a commit) — it now keeps only the person's own edits, the claim rule's own test, applied to its twin; the `Yad-Base` fallback first used the previous capture, which DROPS earlier edits — the opposite of over-reporting; a default branch name origin does not have (no hub default, no `origin/HEAD`) failed the whole `yad claims` fetch — it is now fetched on its own; a monorepo path outside the Product could keep a merged claim open. **Reviews 2–4 found and fixed:** the first-capture search walked at most 1000 commits, and capture branches are never reset — it is now one `git log --first-parent --invert-grep`; that search, and the `Yad-Base` trailer read, depended on the person's own git config (`grep.patternType` made claims vanish, `log.showSignature` broke the hash, `trailer.separators` hid the trailer) — each is now pinned (`--basic-regexp`, `--no-show-signature`, `-c trailer.separators=:`). **No shape change.** **Shipped (#277).** | S | E43 |
| E47 | Step ownership — assign a step to one person — shipped as **`yad assign`**, **`yad unassign`** and **`yad owners`**. The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-25). **Advice, not a lock:** it never blocks an edit, never holds a gate and never refuses a fold. **(1) Storage: one small file per step,** `<epic>/.sdlc/owners/<step>.json` (`foundation/.sdlc/owners/` for the Product level). The finding that set it: `ledger-guard` guards ledger files BY NAME, so a new file is writable on the laptop in both ledger modes, while a key on the step in `state.json` would be refused on a verified Product once the epic's first review merges (as `yad skip` is), and could be dropped by any of the skills that rewrite `state.json` by hand. Two assignments at once on two branches touch two files. **No shape change.** Capture takes the file like any person-written `.sdlc/` file (never counted as a claim). `yad assign` commits nothing, and `yad fold` never takes an owner file — review 1 found that a fold carrying one alone made a `docs: author <step>` commit with nothing authored, and on a verified Product the fold's review branch merges only once the assignment is no longer live; the file is committed on its own. **Review 2 found** that on a verified Product no PR could carry it: the Product's `pr-title` and `pr-template` checks fail any non-review PR touching `epics/` or `foundation/`, and a review/EP-* PR advances the step at merge. **The user's decision (2026-09-26): let owner files through** — both checks no longer count `*/.sdlc/owners/*.json` as a Shape artifact (a full-list read, not `grep -q`, because under `pipefail` an early exit would read as "no artifact" and open the guard). **Review 3 found the exemption opened a bypass:** the hub-checks workflows built the changed list with plain `git diff --name-only`, which names a rename by its NEW path only, so `git mv epic.md .sdlc/owners/epic.json` on a non-review branch listed nothing but an owner file and passed. All four diff lines (GitHub and GitLab, both jobs) now run `git -c core.quotePath=false diff --no-renames --name-only`, so the old path is listed too — and a non-ASCII path is no longer quoted past the guard, an older hole the same line had. **Review 4 found** the rest of that older hole: git still C-quotes a path holding `"`, `\`, a tab or a newline even with `quotePath=false`, and a quoted line never matched `^(epics\|foundation)/` — a story named with a `"` reached the default branch unreviewed. Both checks now read `^"?(epics\|foundation)/`; a quoted line is always an artifact, and the owner exemption never matches one, so it fails closed. **Review 5 found** the two-grep pipe had a hole the old single `grep -q` did not: with `quotePath=false` high bytes arrive raw, and GNU grep (Linux CI) under a UTF-8 locale drops a line that is not valid UTF-8 from its output, so the pipe read as "no artifact" — both greps now run `LC_ALL=C grep -a`. A test runs each workflow's own diff command on a real repo. New bytes in two copied checks and two copied workflows: every wired Product reads `outdated` until `yad update`. `yad update` keeps a file the team changed by hand, so a Product can end up with the new checks and a hand-edited old workflow — the one state where the rename passes; `yad doctor` warns `owners:rename-blind` then. **(2) Effect:** `yad next` prints `owner: <name>` under the step and under its review; `yad owners` lists them; the capture hook warns when someone who is not the owner edits the step's files — the same channel as E46's claims (PostToolUse JSON under Claude Code, stderr under Cursor), ONE note carrying both, at most once an hour per step. `yad next --json` is unchanged (its action keys are frozen by the golden test); scripts read `yad owners --json`. **(3) Identity: the git name made branch-safe by `wipName`** — the name capture branches and claims use, so the hook compares like with like and never asks the platform (the name↔login join is E108's problem). With no `--to` the owner is exactly your capture branch name; `--to` resolves through a capture branch saved under that git name when there is one, so a name with no Latin letters works once that person has captured. **(4) Scope: the authoring steps `yad fold` takes.** Review steps are refused — they share the author step's artifact, and who approves is the platform's and the gate count's to say (Part 3). No Build lanes. **(5) Reassign:** refused while someone else owns the step, naming them; `--force` replaces them and the file keeps `replaced`. The owner unassigns freely; someone else's needs `--force`. A file that cannot be read is refused, never rewritten, unless `--force`. **(6) Ends:** live while the step's work is open — the author step not passed, **or its review not passed yet** (rework during a review is still the owner's work; this reads "the step is passed" as the pair). After that the file stays as a record; re-opening the step makes it live again. No time limit. **(7) Commands:** `yad assign <epic> <step> [--to <name>] [--force]`, `yad unassign <epic> <step> [--force]`, `yad owners [<epic>]`, all with `--json`; the `yad-status` skill prints the owner too, and `yad doctor` warns `owners:ignored` for an owner file that does nothing — unreadable, for a step that cannot be assigned, or not on the chain (it is ignored everywhere else, so the team would not know). **Limits, stated:** two people with one git name are one owner; capture off means no edit-time warning; the file reaches others only when committed and pulled **Shipped (#278).** | S | E21 |
| E48 | Extend the write guard; document branch protection. **The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-27).** **The finding that set the shape:** the "write guard" was `yad hook ledger-guard`, a harness hook — it refused an AI AGENT's edit to the CI-owned ledger, and nothing refused a PERSON's `git commit` of the same files; the Enforcement table's "local git hook" row was never built. **(1) Extend it to people, through git:** `yad hook ledger-guard --staged` judges the files a commit is about to record (read with `-z --no-renames`, so a rename is its old path and its new one), and `.git/hooks/pre-commit` runs it through the committed `hooks/ledger-guard.mjs`. Same file list and same carve-outs as `ledger-guard.sh` — a seed, artifacts, `contract-lock.json`, a merge commit (CI's `diff-tree` judges none), a commit authored by `yad-gate-sync` (CI then checks the signature), and yad's own `gate ci` / `gate repair` ledger commits, which land on the default branch where CI's check never runs (the e2e found the hook refusing a local `gate ci --merged`) — and a test judges eight shapes with both and requires the same answer. Not widened to local mode (the person owns that ledger) or to more files. **(2) Refuse, with a door:** the commit is refused and the message names every refused file, the `git restore --staged` line, the owning command, and the door — `YAD_HOOK_DISABLE=1 git commit …`, said together with the fact that the pull request's check still judges it. The recorded door is E49. Fails open like the harness hook: no node, no guard script, no git, an old `yad` all allow. **(3) The Product hub only**, where the ledger is; verified mode only. **Per clone:** git never commits `.git/hooks`, so `check --fix` / `setup` / `update` install it, it is not in the provenance record (a committed file would tell every teammate the hook is there), and `yad doctor` warns `git-hook` while a clone has none. yad knows its copy by a marker line and never writes a `pre-commit` without it, or into a `core.hooksPath` folder (husky) — it prints the one line to add instead; an out-of-date copy is saved as `.yad-orig` first; with a local ledger `check --fix` removes only an unedited copy. **(4) The docs:** a new page, [`docs/branch-protection.md`](branch-protection.md) — what to turn on in a GitHub ruleset or classic protection and in GitLab, the exact check names to require for the hub and a code repo, solo mode's zero approvals, and how the gate bot's merge-time push gets past protection. `yad doctor` ends a `protection` section that warns with a link to it. **Found on the way:** the `yad-gate-sync.yml` comment said to "add a ruleset bypass so GitHub Actions may push"; a ruleset's bypass list takes actors such as roles, teams and GitHub Apps, never GitHub Actions, so it now says to pass a GitHub App's token or an `SDLC_GATE_TOKEN` PAT of someone on the bypass list. **Limits, written down:** `--no-verify` skips the hook, a clone without it allows everything, and `--amend` is judged against the commit it replaces — CI, made required by branch protection, is what protects the ledger. **Review round 1 (the user's standing order: fix every finding):** the line printed for a foreign hook stopped every commit under `sh -e` (husky) when the guard file or node was missing — every command that can fail now sits left of `\|\|`, and a test runs it under `sh -e`; husky's `core.hooksPath` is `.husky/_`, so the advice names `.husky/pre-commit`; git exports `GIT_DIR` to a hook, which made the base read list nothing for a Product in a subfolder of a linked worktree and waved the mutation through — the base read now drops git's repo variables; the refusal named `yad gate repair`, whose no-push hint then said to commit `state.json` by hand, which the hook refuses — both now say `--push`; an amend that UNDOES a ledger change is refused (git does not tell a hook it is an amend) and the message names the way through; a hook without its execute bit reads `outdated` (git skips it); a `core.hooksPath` naming the default folder is not a tool's folder; and the sentences "could not reach the default branch", "CI still refuses it" and "refuses exactly what CI refuses" claimed more than the check does (it runs on pull requests only). **Review round 2:** the fix reached the commit refusal and not its twin — the AGENT's refusal still said "cannot reach the default branch" and named `gate repair` without `--push`; a test now checks both messages together. Doctor called a hook missing only its execute bit "out of date" and promised a backup it does not make; it now says git skips it. The docs name `yad check --fix --push`, how to unset PowerShell's override, and the subfolder Product whose pull request may carry no ledger check at all. **Review round 3:** clean; its one note — doctor's YAD-STATE-005 hint, older than E48, named `gate repair` without `--push` — now names `--push` on a verified Product **Shipped (#288).** | M | — |
| E49 | `yad commit --manual --reason` override. **Left open by E48; the decisions were the user's (2026-09-28).** **The finding that set the shape:** E48's hook is on the Product HUB, while `yad commit` was the CODE-repo verb (Task trailers, the ≤3-file guard) — it already knew when it ran on the hub, and on the hub it ran `git commit` with the hook live, so a ledger commit through it was refused like a hand one. **(1) The verb is `yad commit --manual --reason "<why>"`** — the roadmap's own words, no new command. With `--manual` its `git commit` runs with `YAD_HOOK_DISABLE=1` (`OWNING_COMMIT_ENV`, the environment `gate ci` and `gate repair` use), so only yad's hook is skipped and any other pre-commit hook still runs. `--manual` needs `--reason`, and a `--reason` without `--manual` is refused rather than dropped; `yad ship` refuses both (the override is its own commit, never folded into a commit-and-open-PR run). **(2) Recorded as a commit trailer, `Ledger-Override: <why>`** — it travels with the commit, the reviewer sees it, CI can read it, and no new file is written from many branches beside the files the guard protects. The reason must be one line (a newline would end the trailer early); the trailer order is `Task → Contract-Change → Ledger-Override → Co-Authored-By`. **(3) CI still fails the commit, and quotes the reason.** Anyone can type a trailer, so `ledger-guard.sh` changes nothing about the verdict; under the FAIL it prints each failing commit's reason, every line starting with the commit's sha so an author's text never starts a line GitHub Actions reads as a workflow command (`::…`; indenting alone would not do it, as review round 3 found — the runner skips leading spaces). **(4) The env door stays**, since yad's own ledger commits and the `--amend` advice need it. The installed pre-commit script's text is unchanged, so no clone's hook reads as out of date. **Tests:** one end-to-end test installs the real hook, sees a hand commit refused with the door named, sees the three bad flag shapes refused with nothing committed, commits with `--manual`, and runs `ledger-guard.sh` on the result — FAIL, with the reason quoted. **Review round 1 (fix every finding):** a lone carriage return in a hand-typed trailer ended the runner's log line, so `\r::error::…` got past the indent — the quoted reason, and the author names the script already printed, now turn every control character into a space; the refusal says `--force` above 3 files and that only a current `ledger-guard` quotes the reason; and the trailer order is updated in its five other copies. **Review round 2:** the cleaning reached the reason and the author name but not its twin on the same line — the file path (a review file named `a\r::warning::….md`, or a new epic's folder in the seed note, which prints on a PASS too) — so every name and path now goes through a one-line cleaner that turns a newline into a space as well; and `yad commit` refused to run from a Product in a subfolder of its repo — where the refusal's `hub:` line points — because it looked for `.git` in that folder; it now asks git. **Review round 3:** asking git for every commit let `yad ship` from a subfolder commit and then fail at the open-pr step, and let a folder inside a parent repo commit into it — so only `--manual` asks git (it also needs a staged file the hook refuses, and `yad ship` refuses it); | E79 | `yad new` · `yad init` · `yad join`. **The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-28).** **The finding that set the shape:** `yad setup` runs INSIDE the Product folder and never makes one, nothing in the CLI created a remote or cloned a repo (only the `yad-connect-repos` skill told an agent to `git clone`), and the workspace already existed without a name — setup's `insideWorkspace` bounds every registered path to the Product folder's parent (issue #129). **(1) Front doors over setup**, which keeps working unchanged: `yad new <name>` makes `<name>/product/`, runs `git init` on `main` (a `symbolic-ref` fallback for git < 2.28; HEAD follows setup's default-branch answer while there is no commit), then runs setup inside it, greenfield unless `--brownfield`. `yad init` runs in the folder that holds the repos, makes `product/` (or uses the one child with a Product config; `--path` names it; two are refused), and runs setup with the repos found beside it — setup's repo step now asks one yes/no per found repo, with the platform read from its `origin` and the default branch from `origin/HEAD`, else the branch it is on. Both refuse to run inside a git repo; `init` points a one-repo codebase at `yad setup --brownfield --monorepo`. **(2) `new` creates no remote.** It prints the lines — `git add`/`git commit` first (`gh repo create --source` needs a commit), then `gh repo create <name> --private --source=. --remote=origin --push`, the `glab` twin, or `git remote add` for no platform. The printed name round-trips: `join …/acme.git` lands in `acme/`. **(3) `join` clones, then per-machine steps only.** `yad join <url> [folder]` clones the Product into `<folder>/product/`, refuses a clone without a Product config, then runs `cloneMissingRepos` — written once for E81 to reuse — over the registry, which is SHARED content, so each entry is judged before git runs: a path outside the workspace, the Product itself (`.`), a missing `git_url`, a URL starting with `-` (and every URL goes after `--`), a non-empty folder in the way. A failed repo clone is reported and the rest continue, exit 0; a failed Product clone is the one fatal case, and a re-run keeps what is there. The per-machine steps: the skill copies the Product's git IGNORES (`git check-ignore`; `--ide-targets` picks the folders) and the E48 git pre-commit hook. A shared skill folder that is missing or old is counted and left to `yad update`. It never commits, pushes, or writes a file the team shares — a test asserts `git status --porcelain` is empty afterwards. Registry text shown in the terminal has its control characters turned into spaces. **(4) `.yad-workspace.json` is E80's.** The README GIF is re-recorded to show `yad new acme`, and its `vhs` script is kept beside it (`docs/media/setup-wizard.tape`) so it can be recorded again. **Review round 1 (fix every finding):** the workspace bound judged the path's TEXT, so a link the Product commits (`evil -> ../../..`) carried a clone outside — the deepest existing folder on the way is now resolved on disk and must stay inside (a repo already there behind a link of the person's own is still "present"); and `--dir` was documented as the workspace for all three, while `new` and `join` make the workspace inside it — the help and the docs now say which, and the printed `cd` lines are relative to where you ran yad. **Review round 3:** both path checks read the TEXT, so a link the Product commits (`g -> .git`) still carried `g/hooks` or `g/modules/<name>` into git's own storage — a cloned submodule git folder with `core.fsmonitor` ran a command on the joiner's next `git submodule update`; the round-1 on-disk check proved only "inside the workspace". | E80 | `.yad-workspace.json`; commands work from any repo inside. **The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-28).** **The finding that set the shape:** no command looked for the Product — every one took `--dir`, else the folder it ran in. From a code repo beside the Product the read-only commands answered "nothing here", and six wrote into the code repo: `update` (the module and CI files), `epic new`, `dial`, `kill`, `skill bind` and `docs sync --wire`. The only walk-up (`hubRootFor`) served the ledger guard, and both hooks already find the Product themselves. **(1) The file holds the Product folder's name only** — `{ version: 1, product: "product" }` in the workspace folder, per machine; the repo list stays in `repos.json`, so nothing can disagree. `yad new`/`init`/`join` write it; `yad check --fix` writes it for a Product whose registered repos live BESIDE it (a Product with none gains nothing), `yad check` says it is missing, and `yad doctor` has a `workspace-file` line. Never written over another file, inside a git work tree, or into the home folder, the temp folder or the top of a disk (a file there would be found from every folder below it). **(2) Product commands find the Product; code-repo commands read it.** One walk-up (`cli/find-product.mjs`) stops at the first folder that is a Product or holds the workspace file. A Product command (a fixed list in `bin/yad.mjs`) then runs against it; a code-repo command (`commit`, `open-pr`, `ship`, `review`) stays on its repo and is handed the Product, whose registry gives a repo run without `--repo` its recorded name, platform and default branch. `--dir` always wins. A workspace file inside a git work tree is not used — a code repo could commit one naming a Product folder of its own — nor one in the home or temp folder; one that names a path, or a folder that is not a Product, is warned about and not used (since review round 1 each of these stops the walk, with the reason). **(3) A writer refuses where there is no Product** — `update`, `check --fix`, `epic new`, `foundation new`, `skill bind`/`unbind`, `dial … --to`, `kill`/`unkill`, `docs sync --wire` — asked after each command's own usage checks, so a mistyped command still hears what is wrong with it first; `setup`/`new`/`init`/`join` are exempt, and read-only commands keep their soft answers. Four tests ran `epic new`/`skill bind` in a folder that was not a Product; their folders now carry a Product config. **(4) One dim line on stderr** says where the Product was found — never on `--json` stdout, never from `yad hook`. **Review round 1 (fix every finding):** a workspace can be a shared folder — setup always allowed `~/Projects/acme-product` with `../acme-backend`, so a routine `yad update` wrote `~/Projects/.yad-workspace.json`, and every other team's repo under `~/Projects` then sent `yad epic new` and `yad kill` to this Product — the file is now used only from inside a repo the Product registers (compared on disk, so a subfolder or a link counts), never from the workspace folder itself or an unregistered repo, and `check --fix` names the file's full path when it writes it; a workspace file that is not used (inside a git tree, home or temp, another `version`) now STOPS the walk with the reason instead of letting it reach a Product above; the lookup starts from the shell's `$PWD`, so a repo reached through a link finds its workspace; `review` from a repo it cannot match borrowed the Product's platform (a GitLab repo would have been driven with `gh`) — it keeps its old answer; a code-repo command matches its repo from any folder inside it; `docs sync --wire` joined the writers (`--refresh` only rebuilds sites the folder already holds); and a sibling repo counts only when its folder is on this machine. **Review round 2:** from the workspace folder itself round 1 answered "Project not set up yet → run yad setup", and running it made the workspace folder a second Product, which then hid the real one from every repo (its workspace file was now inside a git tree) — yad now names where the Product is, `yad setup` refuses a workspace folder, and `yad init` ends with `cd product && yad next`, as `new` and `join` do; `realpathSync` kept the case it was given, so a registry path typed `../Backend` for `backend/` never matched on macOS or Windows — paths are compared as the disk stores them (`.native`), and without case on Windows; `risk-map` and `codeowners` run from one code repo acted on every registered repo — they now take the repo they run in; a git repo nested in the Product's folder that the Product does not register (`vendor/lib`) reached it by walking up — it now needs the same registration a workspace file does; It lists each hand change with a before and after: `SDLC_HUB_CONFIG` set in their own CI settings; their own scripts that open `.sdlc/hub.json` or `hub-prs.json`; the `- local:` include line in their root `.gitlab-ci.yml`; their own GitLab jobs that name a `yad-hub-*` job in `needs:`, `dependencies:`, `extends:` or `!reference`; their own GitHub workflows whose `workflow_run:` trigger names `yad-hub-checks`; status badges in either form (file name or workflow name); `CODEOWNERS` lines naming the old workflow file; tools that read the renamed `--json` names; and the order to run things in (`yad migrate`, then `yad update`, then `yad doctor`). **Shipped (2026-09-30).** As decided, plus the user's calls of 2026-09-30: `.sdlc/docs.json`'s `"scope": "hub"` is written `product` and still read as the Product (no file rewritten, no shape); the skill values `detect-hub` and `repo: hub` become `detect-product` and `repo: product`, the old ones still accepted; a name that holds a file still called hub keeps the word (`oldHubChecks`, `hubPath`, `--keep hub`). The settings object is `productConfig` in the code (`settings` was taken by the Claude hook settings, `product` by folder paths), `cli/hubcommit.mjs` is `cli/productcommit.mjs`. Two `--json` names the row missed moved too: `yad history show`'s `hubWhy` → `productConfigWhy`, and `yad open-pr`'s `stage` (`hub-shape`/`hub-tooling` → `product-*`); with `doctor`'s `ci-tags:hub` that makes six, all in `docs/CLI.md`, and `jsonVersion` is 2 (the golden test's `yad next --json` changes by that one key). The three pattern gates now branch on `product` and turn `hub` into it — the E123 check `productProfileGap` learned that the mapping line is not a branch; its comment holds the whole case table. The one cell the gate check cannot judge (a gate edited after 4.0 to drop that line, beside a workflow passing `hub`) is closed from the workflow side: `renamed-ref:` names every team workflow line that still passes `--profile hub` (user's call after review 1). A code repo may be named `product`, the Product's own scope and label: `items[]`, `commits[]` and the `ci-tags:*` checks gained a `product` key (added, so `jsonVersion` stays 2), `yad update`'s push hint decides by folder, not label, and the skills say to name such a repo by its path (user's call). The `hub.json` keep rule was hiding stale prose that named `hub.json` as the file in use (skills, sites, `cli/errors.mjs`, five printed refusals) — now `product.json`. The docs site template's shell version is 0.0.1 (the overview's `product-hub` component id is `product`), so each per-epic site reads "doc shell upgraded" once. Follow-up after merge (user's call): a sixth gate state, `drift` — when a file's two names say different things every command but doctor/migrate refuses, so the advice says `yad migrate` first instead of `yad update` or a hand fix. `scripts/hub-keep-list.mjs` is the keep list; a test in `cli/test.mjs` runs it, and `yad migrate`'s last line links `docs/migrations/hub-to-product.md` while `hub.json` is on disk. **Blocks promoting 4.0.0 to `latest`** | L | E122 + E123 |

Give them the same judgement (`judgeRepo`, `codeContextPathOk`). `-c safe.bareRepository=explicit` alone is not enough: git before 2.38 ignores the key without a word. **Also (E81 review 6), not git:** `yad repo refresh` runs `npx repomix` (at the vetted version since E88) with the registered path as its working folder, and npm reads a `.npmrc` there — for the Product entry (`.`) or a monorepo subfolder, one the Product commits can point npm at another package registry, whose "repomix" then runs. Run `npx` from a folder the person owns, with `--registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/`, and pass the repo path as an argument; repomix may also read a config file in the registered repo. **Also (E81 review 13):** doctor prints other text the Product writes, outside `repos.json`/`hub.json` — `state.json` step ids, reconcile-debt reasons, capture workflow file names — raw; and `yad risk-map check`'s own `findingLine` prints folder names raw. | **E1** | **`--json` on all ~22 commands, one stable format.** **The row was a bare title, so the decisions were the user's (2026-09-24).** **(1) The format has its own number, `jsonVersion`** (starting at 1), beside the package `version`. Adding a key keeps the number; renaming or removing one, or changing what a key means, bumps it. The state-file `schemaVersion` stays only where the answer IS a file (`yad index --json`). **(2) Flat, with reserved keys.** Every answer is one object: `{ jsonVersion, version, command, ok, …the command's own keys }`; a refusal adds `error`, `code` (the `YAD-` code, or null) and `hint` (or null); `warnings` lists every warning the run printed. The command's own keys sit at the top, as before, so the skills that read `yad dial --json` `.advance` or `yad skill list --json` `.steps[]` keep working. **(3) Break once, declared.** The shapes that moved (a missing `ok`, `schemaVersion` on `yad history`, a refusal without `hint`) change in one release marked as a breaking change, and `docs/CLI.md` lists every moved key. **(4) Every command but `yad hook`**, whose stdout is already a protocol read by Claude Code and Cursor; `yad hook --json` is refused. `yad report --json` prints the scrubbed payload and never posts; `yad setup --json` never prompts and refuses when an answer is missing. **Rules the code already held, kept for all:** exit codes are the same as the prose path; stdout under `--json` holds exactly one JSON object and nothing else, so every other line goes to stderr; a refusal is still one object. **Shipped (2026-09-24).** One emitter, `emitJSON` in `cli/lib.mjs`; a test fails if any other module writes to stdout. During a `--json` run `log` goes to stderr, `warn` lines are also collected, and a refusal said in prose (`fail` + the `hand` under it) becomes the JSON refusal, carrying what the command had already done (a `ship` whose push failed says `committed: true`). A command that answers nothing is reported as a yadflow bug, never an empty stdout; a table test runs every command on an empty folder and on a set-up project, checks one envelope, `ok` against the exit code, and the exit code against the same command without `--json`. New code `YAD-CLI-001` for a prompt a `--json` run cannot ask. **Follow-up done (2026-09-24): a failed docs build exits 1.** `yad docs build` / `deploy` exited 0 and answered `ok: true` when `npm run build` failed (older than E1), and `docs sync --refresh` ignored its rebuild the same way. The decisions were the user's: a failed npm install or build exits 1; a site named with `--epic`/`--overview` that was never generated exits 1 on every build path (build, deploy, refresh); npm missing from PATH fails `build` but is only a warning for `deploy` and `sync --refresh`, because CI builds on push; every site is still tried after one fails, and the run exits 1 at the end. It changes the plain exit code, so it is declared as a breaking change in the same 4.0 release. One `buildSite` says a failure for all three callers, with its hint on the next line, so deploy's later "no Pages platform" line can never become a failed build's JSON `hint`. `--json` gains `sites` per site (`{ site, built, error }`; for `sync` also `stale`, and `built: null` when no build was tried), with `built` kept as the count. Tested with a fake `npm` (and a fake `gh`, for the Pages arm) first on PATH. Its review (one reviewer) found no crash; it fixed a hint that could not name the right site folder, a test that could never fail, a green "deploy via" tick after every build failed, and an error path written relative to the current folder rather than the project. **Seen, not fixed:** `yad docs deploy` never built the `defects-site/` (yad-defects) or `timeline-site/` (yad-timeline) folders, and nor does the Pages workflow; those two skills had said it did, and now say to build the folder with npm and report it as build-only. `list` and the `sync` check only read, so a site never generated reads as stale there, not as a failure. **Left for later:** `yad index --json` answers where `yad index` refuses (a read vs a write; documented) | L | E4 |
| E126 | A handoff view — one short screen per epic. **Proposed 2026-09-30 by the README rewrite; not decided.** **Problem:** a person picking work up, or a new AI session, has to run `yad next --all`, `yad owners`, `yad claims`, `yad thread` and `yad history show` and join the answers by hand. **Foundations:** all five exist and have `--json`. **Missing:** one read-only view that lists what changed since a date, what is blocked and why, who owns each open step, who is editing what, and the next action. **Smallest increment:** `yad handoff <epic>` that only joins the existing JSON outputs, with no new state. **Limits:** claims are read from capture branches, so someone with capture off stays invisible; ownership stays advice. | S | E1 + E46 + E47 |

## Wave 3.5 — The engine drives the agents

| ID | Task | Size | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| E50 | Detect installed skills, agents and MCP servers — shipped as **`yad detect`**. **The row was a bare title, so four decisions were the user's (2026-09-30).** (1) **Two places are looked in: the folder and the user's home folder** (`~/.claude`, `~/.agents`, `~/.cursor`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.codex`, `~/.claude.json`, installed Claude Code plugins). Most people install skills globally, so a folder-only answer would miss most of them. Each item carries `scope` (`project` or `user`) and `where`. (2) **A new read-only command, `yad detect [--json]`**, not a flag on `yad skill` (agents and MCP servers are not skills) and not `yad toolbox` (E86 keeps that word for external tools). It is not a Product command: it runs on any folder and reads no Product file. (3) **Worked out fresh on every run; nothing is saved.** No new file, so no new shape; E51 and E85 call the same function. (4) **Each item: `kind`, `name`, `scope`, `where`, `agents`, `version`, `hash`, `plugin`** — the same keys on every item. `version` comes from the frontmatter (`version`, or `metadata.version`) or the plugin's install record; `hash` is a sha256 of the `SKILL.md` or agent file (E55 builds on it). A Claude Code plugin row adds `commit` and `enabled`. **Rules the row implied:** it only reads (no write, no folder made, no program run, no network — the E11 lesson, and a test compares every file's size and time before and after); **an MCP server is named, never described** — its command, arguments, URL, headers and environment can hold a token, so only its key is read out, and the home folder is shown as `~`; a file that does not parse is a `problem` named by place and reason, and everything else is still listed. **Where each agent looks was checked against its own documentation on 2026-09-30:** Claude Code (skills, agents, `.mcp.json`, `~/.claude.json` user and this-folder servers, plugins with their skills/agents/servers), Codex CLI (`.agents/skills`, `~/.agents/skills`; `.codex/agents/*.toml`; `[mcp_servers.<name>]` and `[plugins."<name>"]` in `config.toml`), Cursor (skills in `.agents`/`.cursor`/`.claude`/`.codex`, in the folder and in `~`; `.cursor/agents`; `.cursor/mcp.json`), Gemini CLI (`.gemini/skills` and `.agents/skills`; `.gemini/agents`; `mcpServers` in `.gemini/settings.json`). Codex does NOT read `.codex/skills`, so a skill there is marked Cursor-only. Copilot, Zencoder and opencode: the project folders yad installs into. **Review round 1 found, all fixed:** the first `config.toml` reader went line by line and listed an element of a multi-line `args` array — a token — as a server; it now reads keys only and skips every value whole (multi-line arrays, `"""` strings, inline tables), and each shape is a test holding a secret. A plugin's `mcpServers` path on another Windows drive escaped the plugin's folder; `local`-scope items were labelled `project` though a teammate gets none of them; the shared newer-version check fetched the registry and wrote a cache after `yad detect` (now skipped for it, as for `yad hook`); `enabled` read only the home settings (now local, then project, then home, as Claude Code decides); the hash counts CRLF as LF, as `contentSha` does (E113), so a Windows checkout hashes the same; Gemini's `settings.json` may hold comments. **Review round 2 found, all fixed:** a plugin `.mcp.json` whose `mcpServers` was not an object had its other keys (`$schema`) listed as servers — now a problem, as in `.cursor/mcp.json`; a broken settings file silently changed `enabled` — now a problem, and read once when run in the home folder; a quoted frontmatter value with a quoted comment after it ran past its closing quote; TOML `\U` escapes are decoded; the update-check skip and the drift skip are now pinned by tests. **Rounds 3 and 4, all fixed:** a `mcpServers` that is not an object in `~/.claude.json` (either level) or a plugin's `plugin.json` is now a problem too, so the docs' rule holds in every file; a plugin's MCP path that leaves the plugin or names nothing is a problem that shows no path; a stray value under this folder's exact path in `~/.claude.json` no longer hides its real entry; YAML quote escapes are decoded, and a value of only invisible characters falls back to the folder name; the terminal output also prints `?` for the characters that reorder or break a line (bidirectional marks and overrides, line separators) and for the zero-width ones. Round 5 added that a plugin naming `.mcp.json` itself, or its own folder, is reported too. **Not here:** which copy of a same-named skill an agent loads (each agent's own rule — the count is printed, not resolved); a folder-level hash of a skill's other files (E55); opencode's agents and MCP servers; Codex plugins' enabled flag and contents; a `yad doctor` line; using the answer in setup (E85) or binding (E51). | M | — |
| E84 | Toolbox manifest — role, installType, version range, fallback — shipped as **`cli/toolbox.mjs`** and **`yad toolbox list`**. **The row was a bare title, so four decisions were the user's (2026-10-01).** (1) **Shipped inside yadflow**, a module that moves with each release like the step catalogue. No `.sdlc/toolbox.json` yet: E86 (`yad toolbox add / remove`) is its first writer and designs its shape. (2) **Three tiers, 13 tools:** core (Repomix, Spec Kit, Impeccable — offered at setup by E85), recommended (BMAD-METHOD, ECC, mattpocock/skills — listed, never pushed) and **connectors**, the adapters `skills/sdlc/config.yaml` already names for design (Figma, Pencil), testing (Playwright, Cypress, pytest, Maestro) and learning (DeepTutor), with the same fallbacks (markdown-only, artifacts-only, harness-native); a test holds the two lists together, in both directions. DeepTutor is one entry, a connector. The docs Pages targets are hosting platforms, not tools, and are left out. (3) **An early read-only `yad toolbox list [--json]`**; `add`, `remove` and `check` refuse and name E86. (4) **A version outside its range warns and the tool is still used.** **Rules the row implied:** it is called the toolbox everywhere — `cli/manifest.mjs` is the INSTALL manifest; `installType` is exactly `plugin | npm | python | script`, and an entry may hold several routes (Repomix has npm and plugin); a tool set up inside a desktop app (Pencil) has no command and links its `manual` steps; **every install command was checked against the tool's own repository or docs on 2026-10-01** by two research passes, with a `checked` date and a `source` per entry; `records` is the exact line a skill writes when the tool is absent (`speckit: not-installed`, `impeccable: not-installed`), and a test reads each skill for it, so E87 renames nothing; finding a tool runs nothing — it reads `yad detect`'s answer (E50) and the `PATH` folders, never `which`. **What the research found:** Cypress and pytest have **no official MCP server that runs local tests** (Cypress Cloud's MCP only reads Cloud results), so those entries say so and link docs instead of naming a community package; Playwright's server name `playwright` is also taken by an unofficial package, and since a server's settings are never read the toolbox can match by name only — the entry says so; ECC's npm package is `ecc-universal` (a test refuses `npx ecc`); BMAD's current install is a plugin or the skills CLI, its older one `npx bmad-method install`; Spec Kit installs `speckit-<command>` skills for Claude Code since 0.4.5. **Review round 1 found, all fixed:** a tool found only through a Claude Code plugin your settings turn off counted as installed (now `disabled`, with its fallback, so E85 never builds on a tool the agent cannot load); the range reader read partial versions (`~1`, `^0`, `>1.2`) and `^0.0.x` wrongly — a range now needs full versions and `^0.0.z` stops at `0.0.z+1`; pre-releases had two rules — now an upper bound always keeps out its own pre-releases, and pre-release labels compare in semantic-versioning order; the connector test checked one direction; an empty or relative `PATH` entry is skipped. All install commands were re-checked by the reviewer against each tool's own source and held. **Review round 2 found, all fixed:** a turned-off Repomix plugin hid the `npx` route yadflow actually uses (now `available` first); a Codex plugin sharing an off Claude Code plugin's name was treated as off (the switch is read for Claude Code's plugins only); on Windows, a `\x` entry (current drive) counted as absolute and a quoted entry did not. **Not here:** any version range — `versions` is null on every entry until E88 vets a default and earns a bound (the range reader is tested on its own); routes outside the four types (Docker, Homebrew, the skills CLI, VS Code extensions), which each tool's `source` README lists; offering or installing anything (E85); a project's own additions (E86); skills reading their fallback from here (E87). | M | — |
| E85 | Toolbox check in setup and update — shipped as **`offerToolbox()`** in `cli/toolbox.mjs`, a step of four commands. **The row was a bare title, so four decisions were the user's (2026-10-01).** (1) **Print only.** "Offer" means print: each tool this Product uses that is missing, turned off or out of range, with its install commands and its fallback — exactly what `yad toolbox check` prints, from the same `toolboxCheck()` and one shared printer (`toolboxCheckLines`). No question is asked and no file is written, so setup does not become a `toolbox.json` writer; `yad toolbox remove <id>` is named for a tool the team does not want. (2) **`yad check` and `yad update` both** end with a Toolbox section (they share `reconcile()`), and its answer joins their `--json` as `toolbox: { used, findings, problems }`. (3) **`yad join` too**, read from the Product it just cloned: a new teammate is the person most likely to lack a tool the team chose. (4) **Setup's preflight line "npx not found — repomix packing will be skipped" stays**: it says what setup itself skips; the toolbox line says how to get the tool. **Rules the row implied:** never auto-install, as E86 already read it — nothing runs a program, and setup never asks to; the step never changes the command's exit code, and if the check itself throws (a home folder that cannot be read) the command still finishes, warns, and answers `error: "could not check"`; setup's step comes **after** the optional-tools step, so a connector chosen there (Playwright by default under `--tools`) counts as in use, and the step count grew by one; a recommended tool is never offered (E84: listed, never pushed) — only what `toolboxCheck` calls in use. **Review round 1 found, all fixed:** `yad check` printed its `--fix` hint under the Toolbox heading, as if `--fix` installed tools (the hint now comes first);
